It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much just the cab. I don't have anything else I feel like I have to do over and over. Like, when I'm at home or anywhere else, I don't check things or have any routines like that. It's really just the car stuff that takes over.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with the safety checks and the thoughts about missing something on the car significantly interfered with your ability to work as a cab driver or affected your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. Um, I've actually lost rides and gotten warnings from work because I just can't get on the road on time. It takes up so much time. I'm also trying to study for my CDL, but that keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by all this. \n\nAnd as for my friends... I barely see them anymore. I'm pretty much always either behind on work or trying to catch up on my shifts to make up for all the checking delays. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things in your everyday life, like money, health, or relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I stress a little bit about money right now, but that's mostly because I'm missing rides and falling behind at work, so there's an actual reason for it. \n\nBut just worrying about my health or relationships out of nowhere? No, I don't really do that. If there's no obvious reason to be stressed about something, I'm fine. Like I said, all my worry is pretty much just focused on the cab.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or uneasy in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I drive a cab, so I'm pretty used to meeting new people all day long. It doesn't bother me at all. And I'm totally comfortable around my friends, too. \n\nLike I said before, the only issue with my friends is just not having the time to actually see them lately because of how long my routines take. But no, just being around people or talking in social situations doesn't make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors when you're not working as a cab driver?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of work. It's pretty much entirely focused on the cab. I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right.

It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. It's just... they're like these intense worries that I can't turn off. Like I said before, I'll know I did the safety check on the cab, but my brain just keeps looping on the idea that I missed something. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. It's just my own brain not letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make these worries about missing something on the car go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I can even take a ride, I... I keep going around the car. I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, my paperwork... just over and over. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much just the cab. I don't have anything else I feel like I have to do over and over. Like, when I'm at home or anywhere else, I don't check things or have any routines like that. It's really just the car stuff that takes over.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with the safety checks and the thoughts about missing something on the car significantly interfered with your ability to work as a cab driver or affected your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. Um, I've actually lost rides and gotten warnings from work because I just can't get on the road on time. It takes up so much time. I'm also trying to study for my CDL, but that keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by all this. \n\nAnd as for my friends... I barely see them anymore. I'm pretty much always either behind on work or trying to catch up on my shifts to make up for all the checking delays. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things in your everyday life, like money, health, or relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I stress a little bit about money right now, but that's mostly because I'm missing rides and falling behind at work, so there's an actual reason for it. \n\nBut just worrying about my health or relationships out of nowhere? No, I don't really do that. If there's no obvious reason to be stressed about something, I'm fine. Like I said, all my worry is pretty much just focused on the cab.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or uneasy in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I drive a cab, so I'm pretty used to meeting new people all day long. It doesn't bother me at all. And I'm totally comfortable around my friends, too. \n\nLike I said before, the only issue with my friends is just not having the time to actually see them lately because of how long my routines take. But no, just being around people or talking in social situations doesn't make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors when you're not working as a cab driver?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of work. It's pretty much entirely focused on the cab. But with the car, it's... well, it's a lot. \n\nBefore I can even take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and my paperwork over and over again. Sometimes I even take pictures of them, and if something just doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right. \n\nIf I'm doing the check and it just doesn't feel right, I get this feeling that I have to restart the whole thing from the very beginning. That's why I take the pictures, just to be absolutely sure. It's like I can't even start the ride until that feeling is settled, even when I know the passengers are standing right there waiting and I'm already super late. I just have to keep doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It's pretty much entirely focused on the cab. But with the car, it's... well, it's a lot. \n\nBefore I can even take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and my paperwork over and over again. Sometimes I even take pictures of them, and if something just doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right. \n\nIf I'm doing the check and it just doesn't feel right, I get this feeling that I have to restart the whole thing from the very beginning. That's why I take the pictures, just to be absolutely sure. It's like I can't even start the ride until that feeling is settled, even when I know the passengers are standing right there waiting and I'm already super late. I just have to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you repeat the safety checks on the cab?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, if I really think about it. It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. Like I said before, I'll know I did the safety check on the cab, but my brain just keeps looping on the idea that I missed something. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. It's just my own brain not letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make these worries about missing something on the car go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I can even take a ride, I... I keep going around the car. I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, my paperwork... just over and over. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I keep thinking somebody could get hurt, or my boss will say I lied about checking it. I just can't make it shut up, you know? It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. It's just... they're like these intense worries that I can't turn off. Like I said before, I'll know I did the safety check on the cab, but my brain just keeps looping on the idea that I missed something. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. It's just my own brain not letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make these worries about missing something on the car go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I can even take a ride, I... I keep going around the car. I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, my paperwork... just over and over. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much just the cab. I don't have anything else I feel like I have to do over and over. Like, when I'm at home or anywhere else, I don't check things or have any routines like that. It's really just the car stuff that takes over.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with the safety checks and the thoughts about missing something on the car significantly interfered with your ability to work as a cab driver or affected your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. Um, I've actually lost rides and gotten warnings from work because I just can't get on the road on time. It takes up so much time. I'm also trying to study for my CDL, but that keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by all this. \n\nAnd as for my friends... I barely see them anymore. I'm pretty much always either behind on work or trying to catch up on my shifts to make up for all the checking delays. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things in your everyday life, like money, health, or relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I stress a little bit about money right now, but that's mostly because I'm missing rides and falling behind at work, so there's an actual reason for it. \n\nBut just worrying about my health or relationships out of nowhere? No, I don't really do that. If there's no obvious reason to be stressed about something, I'm fine. Like I said, all my worry is pretty much just focused on the cab.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or uneasy in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I drive a cab, so I'm pretty used to meeting new people all day long. It doesn't bother me at all. And I'm totally comfortable around my friends, too. \n\nLike I said before, the only issue with my friends is just not having the time to actually see them lately because of how long my routines take. Sometimes I even take pictures of them, and if something just doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right. \n\nIf I'm doing the check and it just doesn't feel right, I get this feeling that I have to restart the whole thing from the very beginning. That's why I take the pictures, just to be absolutely sure. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because... I drive a cab, and even when I know I did my safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me that I missed something on the car. I keep thinking somebody could get hurt, or my boss will say I lied about checking it. I just can't make it shut up, you know? It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. It's just... they're like these intense worries that I can't turn off. Like I said before, I'll know I did the safety check on the cab, but my brain just keeps looping on the idea that I missed something. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. It's just my own brain not letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make these worries about missing something on the car go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I can even take a ride, I... I keep going around the car. I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, my paperwork... just over and over. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much just the cab. I don't have anything else I feel like I have to do over and over. Like, when I'm at home or anywhere else, I don't check things or have any routines like that. It's really just the car stuff that takes over.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with the safety checks and the thoughts about missing something on the car significantly interfered with your ability to work as a cab driver or affected your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. Um, I've actually lost rides and gotten warnings from work because I just can't get on the road on time. It takes up so much time. I'm also trying to study for my CDL, but that keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by all this. \n\nAnd as for my friends... I barely see them anymore. I'm pretty much always either behind on work or trying to catch up on my shifts to make up for all the checking delays. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things in your everyday life, like money, health, or relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I stress a little bit about money right now, but that's mostly because I'm missing rides and falling behind at work, so there's an actual reason for it. \n\nBut just worrying about my health or relationships out of nowhere? No, I don't really do that. If there's no obvious reason to be stressed about something, I'm fine. Like I said, all my worry is pretty much just focused on the cab.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or uneasy in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I drive a cab, so I'm pretty used to meeting new people all day long. It doesn't bother me at all. And I'm totally comfortable around my friends, too. \n\nLike I said before, the only issue with my friends is just not having the time to actually see them lately because of how long my routines take. But no, just being around people or talking in social situations doesn't make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors when you're not working as a cab driver?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of work. It's pretty much entirely focused on the cab. But with the car, it's... well, it's a lot. \n\nBefore I can even take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and my paperwork over and over again. Sometimes I even take pictures of them, and if something just doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right. \n\nIf I'm doing the check and it just doesn't feel right, I get this feeling that I have to restart the whole thing from the very beginning. That's why I take the pictures, just to be absolutely sure. It's like I can't even start the ride until that feeling is settled, even when I know the passengers are standing right there waiting and I'm already super late. I just have to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you repeat the safety checks on the cab?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, if I really think about it. I know I just said it was mostly about getting the feeling right, but... I guess that feeling comes from this thought that just keeps hitting me. \n\nEven when I know I did my safety check, my brain tells me I missed something on the cab. And if I missed something, I start worrying that somebody could get hurt, or my boss will say I lied about doing the check in the first place. I just can't make that thought shut up. It stays in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm at home trying to study. Honestly, that's the main reason I came in to see you.\n\nDoctor: Is the thought of somebody getting hurt the main reason you feel like you have to keep checking the cab?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. That fear of someone getting hurt is really what drives it. Like I was saying earlier, that's exactly why I get stuck before I can even take a ride. I just keep doing it over and over again until it feels precise. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because... I drive a cab, and even when I know I did my safety check, this thought just keeps hitting me that I missed something on the car. I keep thinking somebody could get hurt, or my boss will say I lied about checking it. I just can't make it shut up, you know? It's just constantly in my head the whole time I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study.\n\nDoctor: Do these thoughts about missing something on the car feel like they are coming from outside of your own thinking, or are they more like worries that you can't stop having?\n\nPatient: No, they definitely feel like my own thoughts. It's just... they're like these intense worries that I can't turn off. Like I said before, I'll know I did the safety check on the cab, but my brain just keeps looping on the idea that I missed something. It's just me worrying that someone's going to get hurt or my boss is going to think I'm lying, but it's just so loud in my head. I can't make it stop, whether I'm driving or just trying to sit and study. It's just my own brain not letting it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make these worries about missing something on the car go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Before I can even take a ride, I... I keep going around the car. I check the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, my paperwork... just over and over. Sometimes I even take pictures on my phone to prove to myself I looked at them. And if something doesn't feel right while I'm doing it, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I'm already late. I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge even when you're not thinking about the safety check or driving the cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm actually not thinking about the cab or worrying about the safety checks... I feel fine. I don't just feel anxious for no reason. \n\nIt's just that, well, those thoughts about the car are there almost all the time. But if my brain is actually quiet for a minute and I'm just hanging out or something, I'm not on edge at all. It's really just the car stuff that gets me worked up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do over and over, outside of the safety checks for your cab?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's pretty much just the cab. I don't have anything else I feel like I have to do over and over. Like, when I'm at home or anywhere else, I don't check things or have any routines like that. It's really just the car stuff that takes over.\n\nDoctor: Has your preoccupation with the safety checks and the thoughts about missing something on the car significantly interfered with your ability to work as a cab driver or affected your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. Um, I've actually lost rides and gotten warnings from work because I just can't get on the road on time. It takes up so much time. I'm also trying to study for my CDL, but that keeps getting pushed back because so much of my day just gets eaten up by all this. \n\nAnd as for my friends... I barely see them anymore. I'm pretty much always either behind on work or trying to catch up on my shifts to make up for all the checking delays. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things in your everyday life, like money, health, or relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I stress a little bit about money right now, but that's mostly because I'm missing rides and falling behind at work, so there's an actual reason for it. \n\nBut just worrying about my health or relationships out of nowhere? No, I don't really do that. If there's no obvious reason to be stressed about something, I'm fine. Like I said, all my worry is pretty much just focused on the cab.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or uneasy in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I drive a cab, so I'm pretty used to meeting new people all day long. It doesn't bother me at all. And I'm totally comfortable around my friends, too. \n\nLike I said before, the only issue with my friends is just not having the time to actually see them lately because of how long my routines take. But no, just being around people or talking in social situations doesn't make me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to repeat certain actions or behaviors when you're not working as a cab driver?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of work. It's pretty much entirely focused on the cab. Sometimes I even take pictures of them, and if something just doesn't feel right, I have to restart the whole check from the very beginning. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. Like I was just saying about how I keep going around the car checking the tires, the doors, the lights, the meter and everything... it's more about getting it to feel right. \n\nIf I'm doing the check and it just doesn't feel right, I get this feeling that I have to restart the whole thing from the very beginning. That's why I take the pictures, just to be absolutely sure. It's like I can't even start the ride until that feeling is settled, even when I know the passengers are standing right there waiting and I'm already super late. I just have to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you repeat the safety checks on the cab?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah, if I really think about it. I do it even when passengers are standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late. \n\nSo it's a huge issue when I'm driving, but I don't really do anything like that when I'm just at home or off the clock.\n\nDoctor: When you repeat the safety checks on the cab, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I mean... not exactly one specific bad thing, I guess. It has to be a certain way. Like I was saying before, that's why I get stuck. Before I can take a ride, I go around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and the paperwork. \n\nIf I do it out of order, or if I'm taking the pictures and it just doesn't feel perfectly right, I have to restart the whole check from scratch. I just keep doing it over and over again until it feels precise. I have to get it right, even with the passengers standing right there waiting for me and I know I'm already late.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel less anxious after you've completed your safety checks on the cab?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if I feel less anxious afterward. I guess... I just know that once I finally get the routine perfectly right, I can get in and start the ride. But I don't really know if the worry actually goes away, or if I just feel like I'm finally allowed to drive.\n\nDoctor: Do you think doing the safety checks makes you feel less anxious about the possibility of someone getting hurt?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I don't know if it makes me feel less anxious about an accident or someone getting hurt. I don't really notice a feeling of relief like that. \n\nI just... I just know I have to do the checks. If I don't do them perfectly, I can't start the ride. So I don't really know if it's about feeling less worried afterward, it's just about getting the routine exactly right so I can finally drive the cab.\n\nDoctor: Are your repeated safety checks on the cab specifically intended to prevent an accident or someone getting hurt?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that's the specific reason. Like I said a minute ago, before I can take a ride, I just keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and the paperwork over and over. I take the pictures, and I restart the whole check if it doesn't feel right, even with passengers standing right there waiting and I'm already late. \n\nI don't know if I'm actively thinking about preventing an accident or someone getting hurt while I'm doing all that. I just know I have to do the routine before I can drive."
